# BOM v1

- **Status:** draft (BOM v1.17, cross-validated). v1.17 closes the gates of v1.16 that need no hardware: [Changes in v1.17](#changes-in-v117). v1.16 applies the area swaps of [ADR 0025](../adr/0025-height-zones-in-the-wider-body.md) that pass (0402 resistors and five signal capacitors in 0201); the others wait for their gates: [Changes in v1.16](#changes-in-v116). v1.15 applies the board compaction study (lower inductors, 0201 decoupling, 0.8 mm signal test pads): [Changes in v1.15](#changes-in-v115). v1.14 applies [RFC 0015](https://github.com/opendap-project/rfcs/blob/main/text/0015-hat-connector-pogo-field-and-usb-c-port-hat.md) (the HAT connector is the pogo field with the Mill-Max 0965; a shared limiter for the 5.1 V source; 5 V only from a HAT): [Changes in v1.14](#changes-in-v114). v1.13 applies [RFC 0014](https://github.com/opendap-project/rfcs/blob/main/text/0014-hat-interface-v2-usb-only.md) (hat-standard [interface v2](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/specs/interface-v2.md) and [ADR 0006](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/adr/0006-hats-are-usb-only.md): HATs are USB only, HAT power follows USB PD) to every line that does not depend on the HAT connector, which is still open: [Changes in v1.13](#changes-in-v113). v1.12 applies [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-vdd-cpu-buck-ti-tps62869.md) (vdd_cpu buck TI TPS62869 instead of the RK860-0, I2C0 address 0x42), [ADR 0022](../adr/0022-lpddr4x-micron-mt53e1g32d2fw-with-cxmt-second-source.md) (LPDDR4X Micron MT53E1G32D2FW-046 IT:C, CXMT CXDB5CBAM-MA-B second source, single rank), the board changes of the [HAT flat cable](hat-flat-cable.md) spec (DF40 receptacle on the board, the cable as a specified FPC) and of the faceplate-touch [faceplate flex](https://github.com/opendap-project/faceplate-touch/blob/main/docs/specs/faceplate-flex.md) spec (one panel translator): [Changes in v1.12](#changes-in-v112). v1.11 applies hat-standard [HAT interface v1.1](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/specs/interface-v1.md) and its [ADR 0005](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/adr/0005-interface-v1-1-shared-extension-and-contact-reduction.md) (one DF40C-100 per cable end, 68 pogo contacts, a PCIe/CSI-2 extension through two TMUXHS4412, line out removed, the HAT limiters checked against the new fault ceilings) and the board changes of the t1 [faceplate interface v1](https://github.com/opendap-project/t1/blob/main/docs/specs/faceplate-interface.md) §10 (current-limited faceplate rails, a backlight driver that keeps BL_LEDA below 30 V, I2C3 pull-ups for 400 kHz): [Changes in v1.11](#changes-in-v111). v1.10 applies [ADR 0020](../adr/0020-battery-connected-through-contacts.md) (battery connected through six spring contacts instead of the Micro-Fit 3.0 and the pack lead): [Changes in v1.10](#changes-in-v110). v1.9 applies [ADR 0019](../adr/0019-master-clock-divided-from-twice-the-frequency.md) (master clock divided from twice the frequency), the BOM and power-tree items B1–B16 of [pinout v1](pinout-v1.md) §9.2, and three decisions taken on 2026-10-24 (the radio can stay powered in suspend for item tracking, the faceplate supply is off in suspend by default, the 802.15.4 balls stay unassigned spares); the vcc3v3_sys re-sum passes 80 %, so line 6 becomes the TPS62827: [Changes in v1.9](#changes-in-v19). v1.8 adds the radio's 1.8 V power rail and moves the LPDDR4X VDD1 supply and the VDDQ LDO bias onto an always-on rail , applies the feature-block gaps G1–G21 and folds in [ADR 0017](../adr/0017-fuel-gauge-wiring-sense-resistor-and-configuration.md) (fuel-gauge wiring, 3 mΩ sense resistor) and [ADR 0018](../adr/0018-antennas-and-certification-route.md) (one Molex 146153-0050 antenna, shared configuration): [Changes in v1.8](#changes-in-v18). v1.7 adds the should parts of BRD-057 to BRD-059 (NFC, haptics, a hardware key holder). v1.6: v1.6 applies [ADR 0015](../adr/0015-amplifier-rails-from-vsys.md) (amplifier rails sized for the full-scale peak: LMR33630C −7 V inverting buck-boost from VSYS, TPS65131 boost only, paired LT3045/LT3094), ADR 0014 item 6 (BQ25798 ship FET), the MaskROM fix and two value corrections of the [VSYS buck and MaskROM simulations](../../sim/README.md): [Changes in v1.6](#changes-in-v16). v1.5 applies [ADR 0014](../adr/0014-two-cell-battery.md) (Accepted: two-cell battery with a made-to-order pack; BQ25798 charger, TPS62136 pre-regulator to a 3.8 V VSYS_LV rail, two TPS62903 bucks, BQ28Z610-R1 gauge, 5 A fuse, Micro-Fit 3.0 battery connector) and fixes the board EEPROM address: [Changes in v1.5](#changes-in-v15). v1.4 applies the hat-standard [HAT interface v1](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/specs/interface-v1.md) (Draft; hat-standard ADR 0002 and ADR 0003: low-speed I/O over USB, one HAT USB port, compatibility levels L1–L3, the T1 at L3) to the HAT lines: [Changes in v1.4](#changes-in-v14). v1.3 applies [ADR 0012](../adr/0012-headphone-output-stage.md) (headphone output stage: six OPA1622, ±6 V rails on LT3045/LT3094, CS43198 at `+1dB_EN` = 0 with amplifier gain 1.62) and closes §9 items D27 and D30: [Changes in v1.3](#changes-in-v13). v1.2 applies the maintainers' decisions of 2026-10-22 (power safety first, BRD-049; battery path for the full output budget, BRD-050; RGB status LED, FPT-025; HAT interface by function, HCS-021, HCS-022): [Changes in v1.2](#changes-in-v12). v1.1 closes the datasheet-review items of §9 that a part, value or circuit change can close ([Changes in v1.1](#changes-in-v11)); the rest stay *Open* with their blocker. v1 supersedes the BOM v1 draft after an independent cross-validation (§0). Only lines marked *Decided* are decisions (ADRs). Every *Proposed* line still needs the maintainers' confirmation; *Open* lines have no compliant pick yet; **Blocker** marks a line that cannot meet the product rules today.
- **Date:** 2026-10-27 (v1.17, v1.16, v1.15); 2026-10-25 (v1.14, v1.13, v1.12); 2026-10-24 (v1.11, v1.10, v1.9 and v1.8; v1.7 2026-10-23; v1.6 2026-10-22)
- **Updated 2026-10-24:** stale text aligned with the most recent Accepted ADRs ([ADR 0014](../adr/0014-two-cell-battery.md), [ADR 0015](../adr/0015-amplifier-rails-from-vsys.md), hat-standard [ADR 0004](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/adr/0004-pogo-part-and-field-geometry.md)): line 39 and §3 (BQ25798 binding already in linux-next), line 67 (pogo field), §7 items 3, 15, 20, 21, 22, §8 risks 6, 9, 19, §9 D20, D21 (line references), D31, D36. No decision, priority or requirement status changed.
- **Updated 2026-10-24:** §7 item 1 narrowed by the MCLK attenuator simulation; §7 item 22 narrowed from TI's BQ28Z610-R1 technical reference (lines 42, 43); §7 item 28 closed on Murata and Samsung DC-bias data (line 121). No decision, priority or requirement status changed.
- **Scope:** the T1 main board and the touch faceplate, by subsystem, including the support parts around the main ICs (regulators, switches, protection, clocks, magnetics, connectors, test access).
- **Companion:** [power tree v0](power-tree-v0.md) (updated with this BOM) holds the rails, currents, gating map, sequencing and the calculations behind the power, audio, backlight and HAT picks.
- **Sources:** [BOM v0](bom-v0.md), the datasheets of the [Datasheets](#datasheets) table, [output-stage simulation](../../sim/output-stage/README.md) and [VSYS buck and MaskROM simulations](../../sim/README.md) (v1.6), touch-faceplate ADR 0001, board ADRs 0001–0015, blackbird-os ADRs 0006–0008; from v1.9 board ADR 0019 and [pinout v1](pinout-v1.md); from v1.10 board ADR 0020; from v1.11 hat-standard interface v1.1 and ADR 0005, the t1 faceplate interface v1 and t1 ADRs 0004 and 0005; from v1.12 board ADRs 0021 and 0022, the [HAT flat cable](hat-flat-cable.md) spec and the faceplate-touch faceplate flex spec; from v1.13 RFC 0014, hat-standard interface v2 and ADR 0006, onsemi FUSB302B/D Rev. 5; from v1.14 RFC 0015, the hat-standard ADR 0004 amendment and the Mill-Max 0965 product data.
- **Product rules applied:** availability first and validated; newest compatible generation; mainline Linux drivers (DKMS or validated vendor drivers acceptable); no unusual switches or tricks (BRD-042); helper MCUs never in a data path (BRD-005); audio quality and battery life first; every block power-gated when unused (BRD-008); every bus and interface protected (BRD-007); the SoC reaches every chip on the board directly and every HAT chip through the HAT's USB port (BRD-004).

## Conventions

- **Driver path:** *mainline + Debian* (module enabled in Debian's trixie-backports `linux-config-7.2_7.2.9-1~bpo13+1` arm64 config, re-checked 2026-10-22), *DKMS* (mainline driver Debian does not enable; blackbird-os ADR 0007), *none* (no driver needed).
- **UNVERIFIED** = no primary source found in this session.
- Datasheets (URL, revision, date, sha256) are in the [Datasheets](#datasheets) table at the end. No PDF is stored in the repository.

## Changes in v1.17

Gates of v1.16 item 3 closed without hardware (2026-10-27). Accepted (2026-10-27). Basis: the makers' DC-bias data at each node's highest steady voltage against what each circuit needs (DRV-107), the data sheets cited per row, and the sourcing check (T1-029).

1. **DC bias (DRV-107).** Method: Murata's typical DC-bias characteristic (SimSurfing, `c_dcbias_capacitance`, AC 0.01 Vrms, read 2026-10-27) at the node's highest steady voltage, the lowest value over −20, 0, 25, 60 and 85 °C (the T1 states no board temperature range; X5R is characterised to 85 °C), less the part's tolerance, summed over the positions that share the node; a stated minimum must be met with 10 % to spare (ageing is not in the curves). Where the source gives only a nominal value, the smaller part must keep at least the present part's effective value. Results:

   | Positions | Node, highest steady voltage | Needs | Present part, effective | Candidate, effective | Gate |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | C605, C606 | BQ25798 VBUS, 9.45 V (9 V PD + 5 %) | ≥ 2 µF effective (SLUSDV2C §6.3) | 3.44 µF | 2.69 µF | **passed**: 10 µF 25 V X5R 0603 (GRM188R61E106MA73D) |
   | C608–C610 | BQ25798 PMID, 9.45 V | ≥ 4 µF | 5.17 µF | 4.04 µF | failed (1 % over the minimum): 0805 kept |
   | C612–C616 | BQ25798 SYS, 8.8 V | ≥ 6 µF | 9.28 µF | 7.30 µF | **passed**: 0603 |
   | C618, C619 | BQ25798 BAT, 8.8 V | ≥ 3 µF | 3.71 µF | 2.92 µF | failed: 0805 kept |
   | C901 | TPS62136 VIN, 8.8 V | ≥ 3 µF (SLVSDV2 §7.3) | 1.86 µF | 1.46 µF | failed: 0805 kept; the present part misses the minimum (§7 item 51) |
   | C4402 | TPS62903 VIN, 8.8 V | ≥ 3 µF (SLVSES3A §6.3) | 1.86 µF | 1.46 µF | failed: 0805 kept; §7 item 51 |
   | C2902, C2904, C2908, C2910 | LT3045 / LT3094 outputs, ±6.04 V | ≥ 10 µF ceramic each (LT3045 Rev. D p. 16, LT3094 Rev. B p. 15); X7R | — | 2.21 µF each | failed: X5R candidate, below the minimum; audio supply (T1-040) |
   | C2907, C2909 | LT3094 inputs, −7.06 V | ≥ 10 µF ceramic each (LT3094 Rev. B p. 20); X7R | — | 1.87 µF each | failed: as above |
   | C2814, C2815 | LMR33630 inverting buck-boost input, 15.86 V across | 2 × 10 µF (SNVA856B Figure 3-1), no minimum | 1.97 µF | 1.60 µF | failed: below the present part |
   | C5304, C6502 | TPS22945 outputs (FP_1V8_SRC, MIC_1V8), 1.8 V | ≥ 0.1 µF (SLVS832D §9.1.5) | 0.76 µF each | 0.41 µF each | **passed**: 1 µF 6.3 V X5R 0201 (GRM033R60J105MEA2D) |
   | C1110–C1113, C1126–C1128, C1132–C1134 | RK809 references, RTC and LDO outputs, 0.9–1.8 V | 1 µF each (RK809 Rev 1.01 pp. 10–11), no minimum | 0.76–0.80 µF each | 0.41–0.53 µF each | failed: below the present part |
   | C3517, C5303, C6501, C2111 | Load-switch inputs on vcc_1v8; vcc_ddr at the LPDDR4X | 1 µF (SLVS832D §9.1.4, SLVSEN5B §10), no minimum | 0.76–0.79 µF each | 0.41–0.50 µF each | failed: below the present part |
   | C1334, C1336 | RK3566 SYSPLL supplies | 1 µF 0402 + 100 nF 0201 (Rockchip HDG 1.1 p. 35) | — | — | failed: the source names 0402 |

   Applied: 7 capacitors of 10 µF from 0805 to 0603 (charger VBUS and SYS) and 2 of 1 µF from 0402 to 0201. GRM188R61E106MA73D is 1.0 mm high at most (Murata product data), so it may sit on either side. The remaining 15 capacitors of 10 µF and 16 of 1 µF keep their part.
2. **Lifecycle (GRM188R61E106MA73D):** in production in Murata's product data (SimSurfing product list, 2026-10-27). Sourcing (T1-029), 2026-10-27: pass (was conditional on the lifecycle only).

## Changes in v1.16

On 2026-10-27 the maintainers approved the board's area recovery with the body at the T1-031 maximum ([ADR 0025](../adr/0025-height-zones-in-the-wider-body.md)). Of its part swaps, those whose checks pass are applied; each of the others names what it waits for. Accepted (2026-10-27). Basis: ratings at least twice the node's voltage (the v1.15 rule), the parts' series data, and the sourcing check (T1-029) of 2026-10-27.

1. **Generic 0402 resistors (capture choices, lines 115 and the blocks' passives) in 0201 of the same series:** 121 resistors from Yageo RC0402 to RC0201 (1 %, 1/20 W, 25 V), same values, outside the audio chain (`blk.dac*`, `mclk`, `hpa*`, `audio*`, `jack*`) and the VBUS and charger-input paths (`blk.charger-input`, `usb-c`, `hat-5v`, `usb-vbus-out`); 0 Ω links and values under 10 Ω (current paths) keep 0402. Every one is a pull-up, divider, bias or series part on a node of at most VSYS (8.8 V). Sourcing (T1-029), 2026-10-27: pass (RC0201FR-0710KL as the series' representative).
2. **Five 100 nF capacitors on low-voltage signal nodes in 0201:** C1114 (PMIC power-on), C1115 (PMIC VDC), C1204 and C1207 (vdd_cpu EN and VOS), C1303 (SoC reset), from GRM155R71C104KA88D to GRM033R61A104KE15D (10 V X5R, the part already used for decoupling); each node is at most 1.8 V. Sourcing (T1-029), 2026-10-27: pass.
3. **Not applied, each with its gate:**

   | Swap | Parts | Gate |
   |---|---|---|
   | Audio output stage 0603 → 0402, same series (RT0402 thin film; 100 pF C0G GRM1555C1H101JA01D) | 60 resistors, 24 capacitors | An audio review and V2 THD+N at full output against the 0603 parts (t1 V2-A08; T1-040). Sourcing pass |
   | 1 µF 0402 → 0201 (GRM033R60J105MEA2D) on rails ≤ 1.8 V | 18 | **Closed in v1.17:** 2 passed and applied, 16 failed |
   | 1 nF 0402 → 0201 on low-voltage signal nodes | 6 | No 0201 part sourced yet |
   | 10 µF 0805 → 0603 (GRM188R61E106MA73D, 25 V X5R) | 22 | **Closed in v1.17:** 7 passed and applied, 15 failed; lifecycle: in production |
   | TPS22919DCKR → TPS22916CYFPR (4-ball WCSP) | 6 | A land and symbol in kicad-lib; each position's rise time and output discharge from the data sheet. Sourcing pass |
   | TPS7A2018PDBVR → TPS7A2018PDQNR (X2SON-4) | 3 | Sourcing conditional (authorized stock below one build); the MCLK LDO's TPS7A2031PDQNR has no authorized listing and stays SOT-23-5 |
   | Test pads on power and ground 1.0 → 0.8 mm (line 86) | 115 | A probe check: PCB-D-06's source gives 1.0 mm as the minimum on power and ground |

## Changes in v1.15

On 2026-10-27 the [compaction study](../../hardware/studies/compaction/README.md) checked lower and smaller parts against the design's stresses ([placement v1](placement-v1.md) §3, §4 decision 12). Accepted (2026-10-27). Basis: the study's matrices (BRD-049 margins as power tree v0 §4.10 applies them: design peak ≤ 70 % and the converter's maximum current limit ≤ 100 % of Isat at 30 % drop; heights PCB-Z-01, Z-03; ratings at least twice the rail; PCB-D-06). Sourcing check (T1-029), 2026-10-27: open for XGL4015-102MEC (no listing read at the channels the sourcing matrix reads; Coilcraft sells the line in its own store, which it does not read), conditional for XGL4015-471MEC (one non-authorized channel), pass for the 0201 capacitor; the incumbent Coilcraft parts read conditional or no listing too. Both XGL4015 parts share the land of the 2.1 mm parts they replace, which drop back in (in a band) if the check fails before the BOM freeze. Nothing here is built or measured.

1. **Line 40 (charger inductor) and line 92 (5.1 V inductor): Coilcraft XGL4015-102MEC** (1.0 µH, 4.0 × 4.0 × 1.5 mm, Isat 7.3 A at 30 % drop, Irms 6.5 A; Coilcraft Document 1660-1) instead of XAL4020-102MEC and XGL4020-102MEC (2.1 mm), on the same land; same-land alternate XGL4012-102MEC (1.2 mm). Charger: 3.5 A peak = 48 %, 6.3 A at the ISW rating = 86 %; 5.1 V: 2.14 A peak = 29 %, 5.7 A limit = 78 %. DCR loss +8.9 mW (charger, 2.25 A) and +17.4 mW (5.1 V, 1.6 A).
2. **Lines 106 and 130 (vcc3v3_sys and vcc1v8_wl inductors): Coilcraft XGL4015-471MEC** (0.47 µH, 1.5 mm, Isat 10.5 A at 30 % drop, Irms 9.6 A) instead of Würth 744373240047 (1.8 mm), on KiCad's XxL4020 land (the Würth land was never drawn); the Würth part stays as the other-land alternate. vcc3v3_sys: 2.66 A peak = 25 %, 6.4 A limit = 61 %; vcc1v8_wl: 1.51 A = 14 %, 5.0 A = 48 %. The XFL4015-471MEC of line 106 is 1.6 mm, over PCB-Z-01.
3. **Line 147 (vdd_cpu):** the XGL4012-221MEC is 1.2 mm with the 4020 land (Document 1718 drawing), which closes the land's **UNVERIFIED**; it now stands under the pocket next to the SoC.
4. **Generic 100 nF decoupling (lines 115 and the "capture choice" passives):** 58 capacitors on rails of 3.3 V or less in the core, I/O and radio zones move from 0402 (GRM155R71C104KA88D, 16 V X7R) to 0201 (GRM033R61A104KE15D, 10 V X5R, the part under the BGA). The audio zone and the power zone keep 0402. Their DC-bias capacitance stays **UNVERIFIED** (DRV-107), as for every generic capacitor.
5. **Line 86 (test pads):** pads on signal nets are 0.8 mm (51), on rails and ground 1.0 mm (115), PCB-D-06.
6. **Not changed, per the matrices:** the TPS62136 and LMR33630C inductors (lines 114, 117): the XGL4015 of the same inductance sits at 103 % of its Isat at the converter's maximum current limit; capacitors over 1.0 mm (0805 and 1206): every thinner part of the same value, rating and dielectric is obsolete, not for new designs or not listed (sourcing matrix, 2026-10-27).

## Changes in v1.14

On 2026-10-25 the HAT connector was decided ([RFC 0015](https://github.com/opendap-project/rfcs/blob/main/text/0015-hat-connector-pogo-field-and-usb-c-port-hat.md)): the **pogo field** stays (hat-standard ADR 0001 confirmed), with the **Mill-Max 0965** as the primary pogo part and the Harwin P70 as the alternate (hat-standard ADR 0004 amendment). This version closes the items that waited for it (§7 items 46, 48, 50). Calculations: [power tree v0.8](power-tree-v0.md#changes-in-v08) and the [HAT flat cable](hat-flat-cable.md) spec v1. Requirements: BRD-004 reworded. Nothing here is built or measured.

1. **Line 66:** one **DF40C-50DS-0.4V(51)** receptacle per end (board and device-side piece), the interface v2 §9.2 map, 42 of 46 positions.
2. **Line 67:** **20 × Mill-Max 0965-0-15-20-80-14-11-0**; Harwin P70-1000045R as the alternate on the same HAT pads, with its own piece land and hard-stop height.
3. **Line 102 and the HAT flat cable spec v1:** DF40C-50DP headers; USB pairs only (90 Ω, 72.5 / 100 µm, unchanged geometry, recomputed with `odapkit stackup`); `VBUS` ≥ 18.2 mm on L3 for the 4.5 A fault ceiling; a 22.5 mm body.
4. **Lines 65, 68, 69:** the TVS0500 and both TPD4E05U06 on the device-side piece, at the pogo field; nothing repeated on the board side of the cable.
5. **Line 44: eFuse #D**, a shared limiter ahead of #A and #C (BRD-049). The fault case of v1.13 (both ports at their maximum trip, 3.96 A, 132 % of the 3 A buck) is bounded:

   | Check (BRD-049) | Rating or limit (source) | Value | Use | Result |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | #D trip at RILM 1.47 kΩ ± 1 %, min / typ / max | Equation 7 ± 10 % (SLVSFC9C) | 2.021 / 2.268 / 2.520 A | — | — |
   | Buck (line 91), both ports at their maximum trip | 3 A rating (SLVSES3A) | 2.520 A | 84 % | OK (fault case ≤ 100 %; was 132 %) |
   | Buck, against its minimum high-side limit | 4.0 A (SLVSES3A §6.5) | 2.520 A | 63 % | OK |
   | Buck, design output | 3 A | 1.6 A | 53 % | OK (unchanged) |
   | 1.6 A design against #D's minimum trip | 2.021 A | 1.6 A | 79 % | OK |
   | Selectivity: #A, #C maximum against #D minimum | 2.021 A | 1.981 A | 98 % | OK: an overload on one port, the other idle, trips that port only |
   | Each port's 1.5 A offer against its minimum trip (#A, #C) | 1.589 A | 1.5 A | 94 % | OK (unchanged) |
   | #D switch | 5.5 A (SLVSFC9C) | 2.520 A | 46 % | OK |
   | Buck inductor XGL4020-102MEC | Isat 8.8 A (30 % drop) | 2.52 + 0.54 = 3.06 A | 35 % | OK |

   #D is enabled with the buck's output, its fault goes to expander P16 (`EFUSE_5V_FLT_N`, a spare since v1.13) and its IMON to a test pad. A failed-short #D leaves each port limited by its own eFuse; the 3.96 A case then needs a second, independent fault.
6. **Line 45: 5 V only from a HAT.** The HAT input path is rated for 5 V: TVS0500 5 V stand-off and 7.5 V minimum breakdown (SLVSED2C), FUSB302B `CC` 6.0 V absolute maximum (FUSB302B/D Rev. 5 p. 14), TPD4E05U06 5.5 V stand-off (SLVSBO7O). OV2 gets a divider, **464 kΩ / 100 kΩ 1 %: 5.60–6.31 V**, so TPS2121 IN2 disconnects a HAT that applies more than vSafe5V (a non-compliant HAT: it never offers more than the T1's sink capabilities ask for), and the TVS clamps above 7.5 V. The port controller's sink capabilities list vSafe5V only (power tree v0.8).
7. **Pinout v1:** expander P16 → `EFUSE_5V_FLT_N`. **PCB constraints:** PCB-P-04 at 2.52 A (#D maximum trip).

## Changes in v1.13

The maintainers decided on 2026-10-25 that HATs are USB only and that HAT power follows USB Power Delivery (hat-standard [interface v2](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/specs/interface-v2.md), Draft, and [ADR 0006](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/adr/0006-hats-are-usb-only.md)). This version applies the board change list of [RFC 0014](https://github.com/opendap-project/rfcs/blob/main/text/0014-hat-interface-v2-usb-only.md) §2, except what depends on the physical HAT connector, which is still open (a pogo field on the device-side piece, or a USB-C receptacle on the HAT case side; the flat cable reaches either). **Lines 66, 67 and 102 and the [HAT flat cable](hat-flat-cable.md) spec are unchanged and follow that decision.** Calculations are in [power tree v0.7](power-tree-v0.md#changes-in-v07). Requirements: BRD-045 withdrawn, BRD-006, BRD-013 and BRD-026 reworded. Nothing here is built or measured.

### Removed

1. **Line 38** (HAT 3.3 V buck, TPS62903) and its inductor, **line 7**: interface v2 has no 3.3 V rail. Line 91 keeps the TPS62903 part number.
2. **Line 94** (PCA9306 HAT ID translator): a HAT's identity is in its USB descriptors.
3. **Line 95** (HAT audio translators) and **line 112** (HAT `I2S_MCLK` buffer, Open): a HAT presents audio as a USB device with its own clock, so the DAC clock path loses its HAT taps and the divider question of §7 item 19.
4. **Line 144** (two TMUXHS4412): no PCIe or CSI-2 to the HAT.
5. **Line 135:** ×2 → **×1**. G14 (PCIe reset gate) goes; G3 (MCLK interlock) stays.
6. **Line 141:** the 200 kΩ PCA9306 bias resistor, the `HAT_AUD_OE_N` pull-up and the pulls of the removed HAT lines go; the 4.7 kΩ pull-up on AG37 stays, for `HAT_PD_INT_N`.
7. **Line 93:** expander P01 (`HAT3V3_EN`) becomes `HAT_VBUS_DISCH`, P16 (`EFUSE_HAT3V3_FLT_N`) a spare.
8. **§5 HAT path:** the extension switches' decoupling and RSVD resistors, the `HAT_EXT_SEL` / `HAT_EXT_PD` resistors, the PERST# RC and two of the four AC capacitors go.

### HAT port under USB PD

1. **Line 41: a second port controller, FUSB302B01MPX, for the HAT port.** Same die and package as the main FUSB302BMPX, I2C address **0x23** instead of 0x22 (FUSB302B/D Rev. 5 p. 18, Table 15; ordering information p. 2), so it shares I2C1 (PMUIO2, vcca1v8_pmu, always on) with the main controller and its bus stays up in suspend. I2C4, freed by line 94, was the other option; it sits on VCCIO4 = vcc_1v8, which is off in suspend. VDD from vcc3v3_pmu (2.7–5.5 V, p. 14 Table 8); INT_N to AG37 (GPIO0_A5, PMUIO1, wake-capable; was `HAT_DET_N`) with the existing 4.7 kΩ pull-up. Linux: mainline `fusb302` and `tcpm` (Debian `TYPEC_FUSB302=m`, §3), a second `usb-c-connector` with power role dual and data role host; `vbus-supply` is a fixed regulator on `HAT5V_EN`. Dead battery: with no VDD the part presents its sink pull-down on `CC` (p. 1, "dead battery support"; VUFPDB ≤ 2.18 V, p. 15), so a source HAT can power a flat T1 through TPS2121 IN2. `CC` pins: 6.0 V absolute maximum (p. 14, Table 7).
2. **Line 44: eFuses ×3 → ×2** (#B removed) and **RILM 2.21 kΩ → 1.87 kΩ 1 % on #A and #C.** At 2.21 kΩ the minimum trip was 3,334 / 2,232 Ω − 10 % = 1.344 A (SLVSFC9C Equation 7, ±10 % above 1 A), below the 1.5 A both 5 V ports offer. At 1.87 kΩ:

   | Check (BRD-049) | Rating or limit (source) | Value | Use | Result |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | Trip at 1.87 kΩ ± 1 %, min / typ / max | Equation 7 ± 10 % (SLVSFC9C) | 1.589 / 1.783 / 1.981 A | — | — |
   | 1.5 A offer against the minimum trip, #A and #C | 1.589 A | 1.5 A | 94 % | OK (was 112 %) |
   | #A maximum trip against the HAT `VBUS` fault ceiling | 4.5 A (interface v2) | 1.981 A | 44 % | OK |
   | #A maximum trip against the `VBUS` continuous rating | 3.0 A (interface v2) | 1.981 A | 66 % | OK |
   | #C maximum trip against the main USB-C receptacle | 5.00 A for the VBUS pins together (GCT USB4105 drawing) | 1.981 A | 40 % | OK |
   | eFuse switch | 5.5 A (SLVSFC9C) | 1.981 A | 36 % | OK |
   | Line 91 buck with both eFuses at their maximum trip (software fault; design 1.6 A) | 3 A; high-side limit 4.0 / 4.6 / 5.5 A (SLVSES3A §6.5) | 3.96 A | 132 % of the rating, 99 % of the minimum limit | Bounded below the buck's limit; a sustained double fault is a thermal check for the board model (§7 item 46; was 3.36 A, 112 %) |

   The datasheet row at 1.65 kΩ (1.800 / 2.028 / 2.200 A, SLVSFC9C §6.5) matches Equation 7 with ±10 %. #A is the HAT `VBUS` source, enabled by the TCPM source path through `HAT5V_EN`; #C is unchanged otherwise. Both block reverse current while off (SLVSFC9C, "true reverse current blocking"), so a source HAT's `VBUS` never reaches the 5.1 V buck.
3. **`VBUS` discharge.** SLVSFC9C describes no output discharge, so the HAT `VBUS` takes a discharge N-FET, one more **line 35** DMG1012T-7 (20 V), with a series resistor to GND, gate on expander P01 (`HAT_VBUS_DISCH`, pull-down), driven through TCPM after the source turns off. The resistor value, set by the USB PD discharge time and the port's capacitance, is **UNVERIFIED** until the schematic (§7 item 47).
4. **Line 45 (TPS2121 IN2) stays** as `VBUS` in from a source or dual-role HAT: its 2.95 A maximum trip is 66 % of the 4.5 A fault ceiling. Dual-role power goes through this mux: IN1 (main USB-C) keeps priority; while eFuse #A sources the HAT, `HAT_SRC_ON` forces OV2 high in hardware, so the T1 never sources and sinks the HAT port at once; a power-role swap through TCPM drops one direction before it enables the other (power tree v0.7 item 5). The HAT port sinks 5 V only until the maintainers decide which PD voltages the T1 accepts from a HAT; above 5 V the OV2 threshold, the `VBUS` TVS and the `CC` protection follow (§7 item 48).
5. **Line 65: TVS0500 ×2 → ×1**, on the HAT `VBUS`. 5 V stand-off, 7.5 V minimum breakdown (SLVSED2C §7.4, §7.6); the 5.1 V source sits 2 % above the stand-off and 2.4 V below the minimum breakdown, as since v1.5. A part for higher PD voltages follows §7 item 48.
6. **Line 68: TPD4E05U06 ×4 → ×1** (`SSTX±`, `SSRX±`, 4 of 4 channels). **Line 69: ×8 → ×1** (`D±`, `CC`, 3 of 4 channels). 5.5 V stand-off (SLVSBO7O): enough for `CC` at 5 V `VBUS`; for higher voltages see §7 item 48. Where they sit follows the connector decision (until v1.12: at the pogo field).
7. **Line 70: 100 nF ×4 → ×2** (USB 3 `SSTX` only).
8. **Measurement:** SARADC_VIN3, freed by #B, takes eFuse #C IMON (pinout v1 C10 closed). eFuse #A IMON stays on SARADC_VIN2; the INA236 HAT channel (line 54) is unchanged.
9. **Pinout v1:** 41 HAT balls change (pinout v1, update of 2026-10-25): 23 GPIO balls become spares, the 16 CSI-2 and PCIe PHY balls have no consumer (O2), AG37 takes `HAT_PD_INT_N`, SARADC_VIN3 eFuse #C IMON. USB3 HOST1 is unchanged.
10. **Line 61 (main USB-C):** unchanged part; its eFuse #C takes the new RILM (item 2).

## Changes in v1.12

Two decisions and two specifications changed on 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25, and this version applies their board consequences: [ADR 0021](../adr/0021-vdd-cpu-buck-ti-tps62869.md) (vdd_cpu buck TI TPS62869 instead of the Rockchip RK860-0; Accepted), [ADR 0022](../adr/0022-lpddr4x-micron-mt53e1g32d2fw-with-cxmt-second-source.md) (LPDDR4X Micron MT53E1G32D2FW-046 IT:C with CXMT CXDB5CBAM-MA-B as second source; Accepted), the [HAT flat cable](hat-flat-cable.md) spec §17 (B1, B2, B4, B8) and the faceplate-touch [faceplate flex](https://github.com/opendap-project/faceplate-touch/blob/main/docs/specs/faceplate-flex.md) spec §10 (F2, F3; faceplate flex Draft v0, 2026-10-24). Calculations are in [power tree v0.6](power-tree-v0.md#changes-in-v06). Nothing here is built or measured; no requirement priority or status changes.

### vdd_cpu: TI TPS62869 (ADR 0021)

1. **Line 3:** Rockchip RK860-0 → **TI TPS6286910CRQYR**, *Decided*. I2C0 address **0x40 → 0x42** (§4.2; [pinout v1](pinout-v1.md) AJ37, AK38, and the CSV). Factory boot voltage 0.9 V without software (SLVSFS3B p. 15, Table 8-3); VIN from VSYS_LV; EN from vcc3v3_pmu through 51 kΩ + 100 nF; PG to a test pad only. vdd_cpu stays on in suspend: EN low resets every register (p. 14) and the mainline `tps6286x` driver has no suspend operations, whereas the reference boards switch their converter off through VSEL (cost to measure, power tree §6.5). Same-land alternate TPS628691ARQYR (86.6 kΩ at pin 9, 0x41); second source onsemi FAN53555BUC18X on its own land.
2. **Line 4:** Murata DFE252012F-R47M (0.47 µH) → **Coilcraft XGL4012-221MEC** (0.22 µH). Line 5 keeps the DFE252012F-R47M for the five RK809 bucks.
3. **New line 147:** the converter's passives (ADR 0021 items 3–6): 2 × 10 µF + 100 nF input, 2 × 22 µF converter-side output, the VOS sense network (100 Ω, 100 nF, a 0 Ω DNP local-sense option), the EN RC, the PG pull-up, the A-option resistor (DNP) and two test pads.
4. **Line 137:** the vdd_cpu H5 link sits inside the VOS sense loop, so its 15 mV drop is regulated out; the "0 Ω in every variant" fallback for an RK860-0 without remote sense is removed.
5. **BRD-049 margins (ADR 0021, arithmetic re-checked):**

   | Item | Rating (source) | Load | Use | Result |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | Converter output current | 6 A (SLVSFS3B p. 5) | 3 A (est., power tree §3) | 50 % | OK; 4.8 A before the 80 % rule is reached |
   | Input voltage | 5.5 V recommended, 6 V absolute (p. 5) | VSYS_LV 3.8 V | 69 % / 63 % | OK |
   | Inductor peak against the high-side limit | 7 / 7.7 / 8.5 A min / typ / max (p. 7) | 3.76 A: 3 A + ΔIL / 2, ΔIL = (3.8 − 1.15) × 1.15 / (3.8 × 0.22 µH × 2.4 MHz) = 1.52 A | 54 % of the minimum limit | OK |
   | Inductor saturation against the worst-case limiter trip | Isat 9.9 A at 20 % drop, 14.4 A at 30 % (Document 1718) | 8.5 A | 86 % / 59 % | OK (≤ 100 %) |
   | Inductor heating | Irms 10.9 A (20 °C rise) | 3 A | 28 % | OK |
   | Output against the SoC limits | 0.81 V minimum, 1.2 V absolute maximum (RK3566 DS 1.1 pp. 51–52) | 0.85–1.15 V ± 2 % (p. 7) | 0.833–1.173 V | OK; the top corner is 27 mV under the absolute maximum, at Rockchip's own 1150 mV ceiling; with the 0 Ω local-sense fallback and the 15 mV link drop, 0.818 V at 850 mV, still ≥ 0.81 V |
   | Thermal | RθJA 60.3 °C/W (EVM, p. 5) | ≈ 0.3 W at 3 A (est.; 0.10 W conduction from p. 7) | ≈ +18 °C (est.) | Check on the board |

### LPDDR4X: Micron MT53E1G32D2FW-046 IT:C, CXMT second source (ADR 0022)

1. **Line 8:** Samsung K4UBE3D4AB-MGCL (discontinued) → **Micron MT53E1G32D2FW-046 IT:C**, *Decided*; WT:C accepted on the same land, AAT:C for prototypes once its automotive data sheet confirms the FW package (**UNVERIFIED**). Second source **CXMT CXDB5CBAM-MA-B** after one qualification build; SK hynix H9HCNNNCPMMLXR-NEE removed. The land, the single-rank wiring and the supplies below are shared by every remaining option and go ahead now.
2. **Single rank.** Both parts have one rank: the RK3566 CS1, CKE1 and ODT1_CA outputs of both channels are not connected ([pinout v1](pinout-v1.md) balls A3, 1A2, 1A4, 1A5, 1C2, 1C3, closing its item O14), and ball A8 (ZQ1 on the Samsung part) gets no resistor: **one ZQ resistor** (240 Ω ± 1 % from ball A5 to VDDQ) instead of two. The land takes single-rank parts only; a dual-rank part would need a new decision. Decoupling unchanged.
3. **Footprint and height.** kicad-lib `Micron:LPDDR4_FBGA-200_10.0x15.0mm_Layout12x22_P0.8x0.65mm_Pad0.3mm_NSMD` (kicad-lib `c20ae2d`, `f09fc1a`): NSMD pads **0.30 mm** (were 0.25 mm), sized for both ball diameters (Micron 0.436 mm, CXMT 0.31 mm), same ball positions; the DRAM fan-out is re-checked against the DRU (0.35 mm copper gap, 0.25 mm mask web at the 0.65 mm pitch). Package height 1.0 ± 0.1 mm (1.10 mm in Micron's part chart) against 0.90 ± 0.10 mm: **+0.10 mm on the back side**, for the constraints owner to check against the case's back clearance.
4. **Supplies (BRD-049, voltage windows):** no rail changes. vcc_ddr 1.083–1.143 V worst case inside VDD2 1.06–1.17 V (23 mV and 27 mV of margin); vcc0v6_ddr 0.591–0.609 V (TPS7A1006 ± 1.5 %) inside VDDQ 0.57–0.65 V; vcc1v8_ddr (RK809 LDO8 through the 0.1 Ω link, line 132) 1.79 V nominal at 100 mA inside VDD1 1.70–1.95 V (the LDO8 output tolerance is **UNVERIFIED**; power tree v0.6 §4.8). The VDD1 and VDDQ currents are now public (z42n pp. 50–57) and wait for transcription (§7 item 33).

### HAT flat cable (HAT flat cable spec §17)

1. **Line 66, genders swapped (B1):** the board and the device-side piece carry the **DF40C-100DS-0.4V(51) receptacle**, the cable the two DF40C-100DP-0.4V(51) headers, as Hirose recommends (DF40 catalog, May 1, 2020, p. 19). Receptacle height **1.45 ± 0.15 mm** (catalog p. 4; the header was 1.14 mm) and its land pattern (B4: 0.2 ± 0.02 mm lands, 3.78 / 2.38 mm outer and inner edges, catalog p. 4 note 3). The 1.45 mm height and zone PCB-Z-05 (B3), the net classes (B5–B7) and the footprint swap in the layout belong to the constraints, rules and schematic owners.
2. **Line 102 (B2):** the cable is the 4-layer polyimide FPC of the spec, with its pair impedances, power-conductor widths, stiffeners and bend rule; the headers are counted on it.
3. **Contact count.** kicad-lib's footprint work reads drawing EDC3-311351-00 as 100 contacts plus four hold-downs outside the span, while the catalog note and the spec read the four corner positions as fittings. The allocation on positions 3–98 holds under either reading; Hirose's drawing for approval settles it (HAT flat cable §16 item 1).
4. **BRD-049:** unchanged. The receptacle has the same 0.3 A per contact rating as the header (catalog p. 2), so the line 66 percentages stand.

### Faceplate flex (faceplate-touch spec §10)

1. **Line 96 (F2):** two SN74AXC1T45 → **one**, for the panel reset; the E35GH panel tail has no TE pin (line 81), so no TE translator is fitted on the touch faceplate.
2. **Lines 84, 85 (F3):** both FH12 sit on the faceplate flex's FR-4-stiffened component area outside the display footprint, not in the 1.3 mm gap under the display (ADR 0016).
3. **New line 148:** the board's own SN74AXC1T45 for `WL_PDN_HOLD`, decided in §7 item 40 on 2026-10-24 but on no BOM line until now, so the line 96 change cannot be read as removing it.

## Changes in v1.11

Two interface specifications changed on 2026-10-24 and this version applies their board changes: hat-standard [HAT interface v1.1](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/specs/interface-v1.md) with its [ADR 0005](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/adr/0005-interface-v1-1-shared-extension-and-contact-reduction.md) (Accepted, 2026-10-24), and the t1 [faceplate interface v1](https://github.com/opendap-project/t1/blob/main/docs/specs/faceplate-interface.md) §10 with t1 ADR 0004 (identity and HID transport) and ADR 0005 (supply classes and protection) (interface v1.0, 2026-10-24). Calculations are in [power tree v0.5](power-tree-v0.md#changes-in-v05). The part choices below stay *Proposed*. Nothing here is built or measured; no requirement priority or status changes.

### HAT interface v1.1 (v1.11)

1. **Flat-cable connector (line 66): one DF40C-100 pair per cable end** carries signals and power; the DF40C-50 power pair is removed at both ends. Allocation: interface v1.1 §7.2. The four corner contacts of the DF40C-100DP header are metal fittings and must not carry signal or power (Hirose DF40 catalog, May 1, 2020, p. 10, note 3): positions 1, 2, 99 and 100. The allocation uses positions 3–98 only, **87 of the 96 usable**, 9 spare (9.4 %), none at a corner. The connector is not polarized (p. 10 note 5; receptacle p. 4 note 4): a 180° mating would put `HAT_5V` on signal contacts, so both cable ends are keyed. `HAT_DET#` at position 33 proves the single connector is mated.
2. **Power on the DF40 at 0.3 A per contact** (catalog p. 2; the rating includes the contact's own temperature rise, note 1), against BRD-049 (≤ 70 % continuous, ≤ 100 % at the protective limit):

   | Rail | Contacts | Continuous (interface rating) | At the interface fault ceiling | At the T1's own maximum trip (items 6, 7) |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | `HAT_5V` | 10 | 2.0 A → 0.20 A (67 %) | 3.0 A → 0.30 A (100 %) | out 1.68 A → 0.17 A (56 %); in 2.95 A → 0.295 A (98 %) |
   | `HAT_3V3` | 5 | 1.0 A → 0.20 A (67 %) | 1.5 A → 0.30 A (100 %) | 1.16 A → 0.23 A (77 %) |
   | `GND` (power return) | 15 | 3.0 A → 0.20 A (67 %) | 4.5 A → 0.30 A (100 %) | 2.95 + 1.16 = 4.11 A → 0.27 A (91 %) |

   `HAT_5V` out and in never flow together (TPS2121 OV2 interlock, line 45). Contact resistance ≤ 90 mΩ (catalog p. 2): 30 power contacts at 0.20 A dissipate ≤ 0.11 W per connector (arithmetic).
3. **Pogo field (line 67): 100 → 68 contacts** (4 × 17 at 2.54 mm, 40.64 × 7.62 mm span); spring force ≈ 60 N with the P70 (0.88 N each), ≈ 77 N with the Mill-Max 0965 second source (unchanged).
4. **ESD (lines 68, 69).** High-speed: **TPD4E05U06 × 6 → × 4** for 8 pairs (USB 3 SS × 2, USB 2, `EXT0`–`EXT4`), 16 lines on 16 channels. Low-speed: **TPD4E05U06 × 8** for 30 lines (22 low-speed and 8 reserved, all with cable positions) on 32 channels; **TPD1E10B06 × 2 → × 0**: line out leaves the interface (ADR 0005 item 5). The board never had a line-out source (no DAC output or ball was assigned to it); only its two ESD parts and the interface positions existed, and both are gone.
5. **Extension switch (new line 144): two TI TMUXHS4412IRUAR.** `EXT0`–`EXT4` (common ports) select between port A (PCIe REFCLK, TX, RX on `EXT0`–`EXT2`) and port B (CSI-2 D0, CLK, D1, D2, D3 on `EXT0`–`EXT4`). Control from the two balls freed by the dedicated SWD lines (pinout v1, changes of 2026-10-24): `HAT_EXT_SEL` and `HAT_EXT_PD`. The PCIe TX AC capacitors (line 70) sit between the SoC and port A, never on the shared path, because CSI-2 is DC-coupled. **BRD-042** (reworded 2026-10-24: a standard switch is acceptable when it keeps the full rate with margin), typical values from TI SLASEW5A §5.6–5.7 (no minimum or maximum is given):

   | | PCIe Gen2 (5 GT/s, Nyquist 2.5 GHz) | D-PHY at 2.5 Gb/s per lane (Nyquist 1.25 GHz) |
   |---|---|---|
   | −3 dB bandwidth 13 GHz | 5.2 × Nyquist | 10.4 × Nyquist |
   | Insertion loss | −0.7 dB at 2.5 GHz | between −0.4 dB (10 MHz) and −0.7 dB (2.5 GHz) |
   | Return loss | −23 dB at 2.5 GHz | between −30 dB (10 MHz) and −23 dB (2.5 GHz) |
   | Crosstalk / off isolation | −64 dB / −27 dB at 2.5 GHz | between the 10 MHz (−73 dB / −57 dB) and 2.5 GHz rows |
   | Skew, intra-pair and inter-pair | 4 ps each, 2 % of the 200 ps unit interval | 4 ps, 1 % of the 400 ps unit interval |
   | Common mode, swing | 0–1.8 V at VCC 3.3 V, ≤ 1.8 Vpp differential | covers D-PHY low-power signalling (**UNVERIFIED** against the MIPI D-PHY text, member-only) |

   The part is listed by TI for PCIe 1.0–4.0 and MIPI DSI/CSI (§7.1), so both links run at the most the RK3566 offers through it. The loss of the whole channel (switch, DF40, flat cable, pogo field) is **UNVERIFIED** until the hat-standard coupon.
6. **eFuse maximum trips (line 44)** with the RILM tolerance (BRD-049; interface v1.1 §5, "maximum trip current, tolerance included, inside the fault ceiling"): #A and #C 2.21 kΩ 1 % → 3,334 / 2,188 Ω = 1.524 A typical (SLVSFC9C Equation 7) + 10 % (§6.5, ILIM > 1 A) = **1.68 A**, 56 % of the 3.0 A `HAT_5V` ceiling (was quoted 1.66 A without the resistor tolerance); #B 3.32 kΩ 1 % → 1.150 A × 3.320 / 3.287 = **1.16 A**, 77 % of the 1.5 A `HAT_3V3` ceiling. No change of part or value.
7. **TPS2121 current limit (line 45): RILM 44.2 kΩ → 45.3 kΩ 0.1 %.** TI characterises the TPS2121 limit at five resistor values, all with a ±0.5 A spread (±0.6 A at 18.7 kΩ; SLVSEA3F §7.5). At 44.2 kΩ 1 % the maximum is 2.52 + 0.5 = **3.02 A, above the 3.0 A ceiling**. With 45.3 kΩ 0.1 %: ILM = 65.2 / R^0.861 (Equation 2) = 2.45 A typical, **2.95 A maximum (98 % of 3.0 A)**, 1.94 A minimum, taking the same ±0.5 A spread at 45.3 kΩ (**UNVERIFIED**: TI has no row at that value; measure on the first boards). Because the minimum is now below 2.0 A, the BQ25798 input limit IINDPM is set to **1.9 A** (was 2.0 A) whenever VBUS ≤ 5.5 V, so the mux never limits during normal charging: IINDPM regulates at or below its setting (1.80–1.96 A at a 2.0 A setting, SLUSDV2C §6.5, measured at 9 V), ≈ 1.86 A maximum at 1.9 A (scaled, est.), 96 % of the mux minimum. 9 V PD input stays at 1.67 A (15 W). This costs 0.5 W of 5 V charging input (10 → 9.5 W).
8. **Flat cable (line 102):** one DF40C-100 at each end; shared `EXT` pairs at 100 Ω differential, USB 3 at 90 Ω; power conductors sized for the fault ceilings (a limiter in current limit holds its current until thermal shutdown): IPC-2221 external, ΔT 10 °C, `HAT_5V` 3.0 A 1.37 mm at 35 µm (2.66 mm at 18 µm), `HAT_3V3` 1.5 A 0.53 mm (1.02 mm), `GND` 4.5 A 2.39 mm (4.65 mm) or a full ground layer, which also references the pairs (arithmetic; FPC copper and stackup **UNVERIFIED** until the FPC fab confirms them).
9. **Sidebands (line 135, pinout v1):** the G14 gate's output becomes `HAT_EXT_SB0` (PCIe PERST# or the sensor reset, both held asserted until HAT power is good); `HAT_EXT_SB1` and `HAT_EXT_SB2` are the PCIe CLKREQ#/WAKE# balls, used as GPIO in CSI-2 mode, with their 4.7 kΩ pull-ups. SWD to the HAT becomes a bit-banged alternate on `HAT_GPIO2` (SWCLK) and `HAT_GPIO3` (SWDIO).

### Faceplate interface v1 (v1.11)

From the t1 faceplate interface §10, BC1–BC8; contact positions unchanged.

1. **FP_3V3 (BC1; new line 145): TPS22919 → TI TPS22950YBHR**, a current-limited load switch, RILIM **3.57 kΩ 1 %**: ILIM = 1.18 × R^−1.072 (SLVSFJ2B Equation 1) = 0.30 A typical. TI gives the spread at 2.21 kΩ (0.38–0.62 A, ±24 %) and 19.2 kΩ (±32 %); interpolating on log R gives ±26 % at 3.57 kΩ (**UNVERIFIED**, measure): **0.22–0.38 A** with the resistor tolerance, the maximum 96 % of the 0.4 A ceiling and the minimum above the 200 mA budget. Auto-retry on overcurrent, reverse-current blocking, quick output discharge, 1.8–5.5 V, 62 mΩ maximum at 3.3 V (SLVSFJ2B §7.5). The SOT-23 TPS22950C is not usable: its range starts at 0.5 A (Table 5-1).
2. **FP_1V8, MIC_1V8 (BC2) and FP_VCI (BC3; new line 146): three TI TPS22945DCKR**, fixed current limit **100–200 mA** (SLVS832D §7.5, at VIN 3.3 V), 10 ms blanking and 80 ms auto-restart, active-high ON, 1.62–5.5 V: the maximum is 100 % of the 0.2 A ceiling and the minimum is at the FP_1V8 and FP_VCI budgets (100 mA) and five times the MIC_1V8 budget (20 mA). TI specifies the limit at 3.3 V only; at 1.8 V and 2.8 V it is **UNVERIFIED** (TI query, or a measurement across parts on the M-UI module). Its ON resistance is up to 1.1 Ω at 1.8 V (≈ 0.8 Ω at 2.8 V, interpolated): at the 100 mA budget FP_1V8 drops 0.11 V; the touch faceplate draws ≈ 5 mA (5.5 mV). Alternate on MIC_1V8: TPS22946 with CL low (70–120 mA characterised at 1.8 V, SLVS984B §6.5; WCSP). TPS22919 count (line 50): **9 → 6**.
3. **FP_VCI (BC3).** The RK809 datasheet (Rev 1.01) rates LDO4 at 100 mA (Table 4-1, RK809-1) or 400 mA (electrical table, `RegLDO4_MAX` = 1) and offers only `LDO4_IMAX` (0x00D2 bit 7: normal or 130 % of nominal, p. 86), with no trip value: **LDO4's limit cannot be confirmed ≤ 0.2 A**, so FP_VCI gets one of the TPS22945 of item 12 between LDO4 and contact 44, ON from `FP_IO_EN`. **Power-up state:** the RK809-1 starts LDO4 at 3.0 V in slot 5 of its sequence (Table 4-1); the RK809-5 OTP table is not public (§9 D19). With the switch, FP_VCI stays off at the contact until `FP_IO_EN` (expander P00, pulled down) goes high, whatever the OTP does.
4. **Backlight (BC4; lines 51–53): TPS61169 → TI TPS61160DRVR.** Its open-LED threshold is **25–27 V** at the SW pin (SLVS791E §6.5), so BL_LEDA stays below 30 V in every state, an open string included; the TPS61169's 36–39 V did not. A faceplate string's forward voltage stays ≤ 22 V (the 25 V minimum threshold less 3 V; faceplate interface §2.3). RSET 200 mV / 60 mA → **3.32 Ω** (60.2 mA). Inductor **10 → 22 µH**, Murata LQH3NPN220MMEL on the same 3 × 3 mm land (0.54 A rating from a distributor listing, Murata sheet not retrieved): TI recommends 10–22 µH (§8.2.1.2.2), and with 10 µH the worst-case maximum output at VIN 3.8 V is 51 mA, below the 60 mA full scale. With 22 µH, worst case (ILIM 0.56 A minimum, 500 kHz minimum, η 0.85 est., SLVS791E Equations 3–4): **82 mA at a 16.5 V string (60 mA = 73 %)**, 75 mA at 18 V (80 %), 61 mA at 22 V (99 %); inductor peak 0.44 A at 16.5 V and 60 mA (82 % of 0.54 A). A faceplate whose string exceeds 18 V declares a full-scale current that keeps 80 %, until a driver on VSYS is studied (power tree §8). Rectifier PMEG6010CEH unchanged (60 V against 27 V); output capacitor ≥ 35 V (the 50 V part stays). New land: WSON-6 2 × 2 mm. Software unchanged: `pwm-backlight` on CTRL (PWM 5–100 kHz, §6.3).
5. **Faceplate I2C3 pull-ups (BC6):** 4.7 kΩ (the §5 3.3 V default) → **2.2 kΩ to FP_3V3**. Fast mode needs tr ≤ 300 ns; with the faceplate's 100 pF and ≈ 20 pF on the board (SoC pad, ESD channel, trace; est.) Rp ≤ 300 ns / (0.8473 × 120 pF) = 2.95 kΩ and Rp ≥ (3.3 − 0.4 V) / 3 mA = 0.97 kΩ (NXP UM10204 §7.1); 2.2 kΩ gives 224 ns (75 %). 4.7 kΩ gave 478 ns, outside fast mode.
6. **Identity controller (BC5, BC7).** The faceplate's HID-over-I2C identity device (0x2C, descriptor register 0x0001, `HID_INT#` level-low) needs no board part: the board devicetree node is in the [feature blocks](feature-blocks.md) (`blk.fp-controls`). The identity controller's BOM line belongs to faceplate-touch (BC7), not here.
7. **Naming (BC8):** "faceplate interface v0" → "faceplate interface v1" across the board specifications; no contact moves.

## Changes in v1.10

[ADR 0020](../adr/0020-battery-connected-through-contacts.md) (Accepted). Calculations are in [power tree v0.4](power-tree-v0.md#changes-in-v04) §4.7.1. Nothing here is built or measured; no requirement priority or status changes.

1. **Battery connection (line 47).** Molex Micro-Fit 3.0 43650-0400 → **six KYOCERA AVX 70-9258-001-120-006** spring contacts in a window of the pocket floor at the pocket's bottom end, face compression on the pack's pads. Order at 3.5 mm pitch: **P+, P+, empty position, mid-cell tap, NTC, P−, P−**. Two contacts per power pole; no Kelvin contacts (ADR 0020 item 4). Alternates: none on the same land; Mill-Max 0965 or 0990 pogo pins, four per pole on their own land, to qualify.
2. **Pack specification (line 46).** The 4-pin Micro-Fit 3.0 lead becomes a **terminal board with recessed hard-gold pads**: seven positions (P+, P+, no pad, mid-cell tap, NTC, P−, P−), pads ≥ 4.0 × 2.5 mm, electrolytic hard gold ≥ 0.76 µm over nickel, power pads recessed 0.2 mm and signal pads 0.4 mm, walled against a flat metal object, **keyed** by an asymmetric terminal end matching the pocket and polarity-marked; ≥ 5 A per power pad; the PCM's short-circuit threshold, delay and behaviour under the insertion pulse stated; UN 38.3 and IEC 62133-2 on the pack as shipped with exposed pads; a terminal cover for spares (ADR 0020 item 8). The acceptance criterion "lead and plug ≥ 4.6 A per pole" becomes "contact interface ≥ 4.6 A per pole" (ADR 0020 item 3). Keying moves from the Micro-Fit plug to the pack's terminal end and the pocket.
3. **Wording (lines 39, 42, 48, 104).** "Battery connector" → "battery contacts" (line 47): the gauge VC2 and VC1 and the charger TS take the pack through the contacts; the fuse sits between the battery contacts and the BQ25798 BAT pins.
4. **BATP network (line 115).** BATP 100 Ω in series (already listed) **and 47 nF to GND**, TI's recommendation for hot-plugged packs (SLUSDV2C Figure 8-2 note 2; ADR 0020 item 6). Test points on the pack side of the fuse and on BAT for the insertion measurement (CB-07, PCB-L-09) add two to line 86 (PCB-T-02).
5. **Battery-contact protection (new line 142).** One TPD1E10B06DPYR on each of the mid-cell tap and NTC contacts, within 3 mm of the contact (PCB-L-08), and an **unfitted P+ TVS footprint** (standoff ≥ 9 V, clamp < 20 V, part to choose), fitted only if the insertion and ESD measurements (CB-07, CB-10) ask for it.
6. **Cover detect (new line 143, should).** A board input that changes state when the back cover is lifted, so Blackbird OS powers down in order (the ADR 0017 item 3 sequence when no adapter is present) before the pack can be reached: a board-edge contact pad for the case's actuator, a pull-up and one TPD1E10B06DPYR; the GPIO is chosen in the pinout. It protects data, not safety (T1-086). Block `blk.cover-detect` in the [feature-block catalog](feature-blocks.md); variant field per RFC 0011: fitted in `full` and `production`.
7. **BRD-049 margins of the battery path through the contacts** (power tree §4.7.1). Two contacts per pole with a sharing factor of 1.25 on the hotter contact (assumed until CB-01): worst continuous 3.2 A → 2.0 A = **33 %** of 6 A (≤ 70 % contact rule; Micro-Fit was 64 %); full budget 2.4 A → 25 %; 5 A protective limit (PCM trip upper limit, fuse rating) → 3.1 A = **52 %** (≤ 100 % trip rule); pole rating 0.8 × 2 × 6 A = 9.6 A against ADR 0014's ≥ 4.6 A per pole; one contact of a pole not touching (a fault, not a design case): 53 % at 3.2 A, 83 % at 5 A. **Heat**, with an end-of-life resistance ≤ 20 mΩ per contact (**UNVERIFIED** until KYOCERA AVX product specification 201-01-252 or CB-01): 0.08 W on the hotter contact and ≈ 0.26 W in the four power contacts at 3.2 A (80 mV in total); 0.19 W and ≈ 0.63 W at 5 A for the PCM's trip time only. Temperature rise **UNVERIFIED** (no published thermal resistance; CB-02). Path resistance ≈ 65 mΩ at end of life (est.; was ≈ 38–48 mΩ): the cell pair holds ≈ 6.8 V for VSYS = 6.6 V at 3.2 A.
8. **Pocket length (§7 item 42).** The 2.5 mm of pocket length budgeted for the lead exit, bend radius and connector seating is no longer needed: it returns to the pack (about +4 % of pack length, arithmetic 2.5 / 60.5, est.) or to the case. Any re-run of the pack-size arithmetic takes the pocket length without that allowance; the pocket floor gains the contact window (about 24.5 × 9 mm, arithmetic) with an insulating liner, and the six contact forces become an input to the back cover's foam preload.
9. **Area (est.):** the window takes about 24.5 × 9 mm from the zone under the pocket (RK3566, LPDDR4X, eMMC), an input to placement (PCB-L-04).

## Changes in v1.9

[ADR 0019](../adr/0019-master-clock-divided-from-twice-the-frequency.md) (Accepted), the BOM and power-tree items B1–B16 of [pinout v1](pinout-v1.md) §9.2, and three decisions taken on 2026-10-24. Calculations are in [power tree v0.3](power-tree-v0.md#changes-in-v03). Nothing here is built or measured; no requirement priority or status changes.

1. **Master clock ([ADR 0019](../adr/0019-master-clock-divided-from-twice-the-frequency.md); lines 20, 21, 29, 139, 140, 112, 135; §4.3; §7 items 1, 10, 19).** The oscillators become **NDK NSC5083D-45.158M** and **NSC5083D-49.152M** (same NZ2520SDA land, MCLK_3V1 unchanged), one shared net with 33 Ω at each output and a 100 kΩ pull-down at the dividers. **One TI SN74AUC1G74DCUR per DAC** (new line 139) divides by two, so the XTI duty no longer depends on the oscillator's 40–60 % symmetry (simulated 45.08–54.35 % against 45–55 %). The dividers run from a new rail **MCLK_1V8**: a third TPS7A2018 (line 29) from AUDIO_2V2 with EN tied to DAC_DIG_1V8. The passive attenuator of §7 item 1 is removed; its values never became BOM lines. Both divider CLR̅ inputs sit on `MCLK_DIV_RUN` (100 kΩ pull-down); pinout v1 has no ball for it (§7 item 39). The HAT copy (line 112) cannot hang on the oscillator net, which has 1.3 pF left of its 15 pF rating. Power: ≈ 8.9 mA at 1.8 V (≈ 20 mW at AUDIO_2V2) for the dividers, minus the attenuator's ≈ 6.5 mW: ≈ +13 mW in playback (est., ADR 0019).
2. **Radio in suspend for item tracking (decision 2026-10-24; BRD-030 should, T1-069; lines 50, 55, 93, 103, 129; §7 item 40).** The Type 2EL can stay powered in suspend so Bluetooth item tracking keeps running. Both module power rails are switched from the always-on domain: AVDD33 (vcc3v3_wl) moves from RK809 SWOUT1 to a **ninth TPS22919** from vcc3v3_sys, ON from **expander P06 `WL_PWR_EN`** (a spare in pinout v1; the expander holds its outputs through suspend; board pull-down, so off by default); the AVDD18 buck (line 129) keeps its EN on vcc3v3_wl, so one line switches both. Default: off in suspend; on in suspend only while the listener enables tracking. BRD-049: 1.05 A peak = 70 % of the switch's 1.5 A, 0.24 A typical = 16 %; 95 mV across 90 mΩ at the peak. SWOUT1 is left unused. VIO and SD_VIO stay on vcc_1v8 (VCCIO4, as pinout v1 confirms); while tracking is enabled, vcc_1v8 also stays on in suspend (RK809 sleep configuration, no part), so no module output drives an unpowered VCCIO4 pad. Suspend draw: **UNVERIFIED** until measured on the radio module (M-RF) through links 11 and 12 (power tree §4.12). `WL_PDN` (GPIO2_B7) loses its state in Rockchip's standby (HDG p. 35): for the pinout owner (§7 item 40).
3. **Faceplate supply in suspend (decision 2026-10-24; T1-060).** FP_3V3 and FP_1V8 are off in suspend by default; the T1 wakes from the body buttons on the always-on domain (power key on RK809 PWRON, Action button on a PMUIO2 ball). `FP_IO_EN` stays on expander P00, which holds its state through suspend, so the hardware can keep FP_3V3 on for a faceplate that must wake the T1 (`FP_HID_INT_N` on its PMUIO1 wake ball, masked while FP_3V3 is off; pinout v1). FP_1V8 comes from vcc_1v8 and follows it. No part changes; pinout B5 closed.
4. **802.15.4 balls (decision 2026-10-24; T1-077).** The four 1.8 V SPI2 M0 balls (GPIO2_C1–C4) stay unassigned spares reserved for a variant: no requirement asks for 802.15.4, and T1-077 keeps it disabled while it is not wired. The module's 802.15.4 SPI (pins 4–8) stays unconnected (line 55). Pinout O6 closed.
5. **vcc3v3_sys re-sum (BRD-049; line 6, 106; §7 item 36).** Estimated peak 2.45 A: BUCK5 input 0.40, RK809 LDOs 0.30, radio AVDD33 1.05 (now through the line 50 switch, same rail), SWOUT2 0.20, eMMC 0.25, haptic driver 0.10 (line 123, missing from the v1.8 sum), the rest 0.15 A. That is 82 % of the TPS62826's 3 A, past 80 %, so line 6 becomes the pin-compatible **TI TPS62827DMQR** (4 A): **61 %**; inductor peak 2.66 A = 55 % of its 4.8 A minimum high-side limit; its 6.4 A maximum limit is 94 % of TI's 6.8 A figure for the line 106 inductor. Output capacitance ≥ 20 µF effective (SLVSEF9I p. 14): the 22 µF + 10 µF of D26 stays, checked on the maker's DC-bias curve at schematic capture. The TPS62826 stays on line 129.
6. **Pinout v1 items (§9.2 B1–B16).** B1: `vccio6_1v8`, the audio IO bank on its own branch of vcc_1v8 through a 0 Ω link with a ferrite-bead option and 100 nF per supply ball (line 141; power tree §3). B2: IO-domain table in power tree §3.1. B3: `HAT3V3_EN` on expander P01 (line 38). B6: eFuse IMON #A to SARADC_VIN2, #B to VIN3, #C to a test pad (line 44). B7: no gauge alert line (line 42). B8: a fourth DMG1012T-7 drives the RP2354A BOOTSEL (line 35); PCA9306 EN/VREF2 200 kΩ to HAT_3V3; `HAT_AUD_OE_N` pull-up; 0 Ω footprints on both ST lines; the MAX98357A SD_MODE select resistor; the `USB_OTG0_VBUSDET` divider (line 141). B9, B10: pulls and series resistors (line 141, §5). B11: expander port map (line 93). B12: RK809 codec pins (line 2). B13: §4.2 replaced by the pinout v1 domain map. B14: AXC translators' B side and PCA9306 VREF2 on HAT_3V3 (lines 94, 95). B15: BOOTSEL and RUN (line 12). B16: USB PHY 3.3 V supplies on vcc3v3_sys (power tree §3, **UNVERIFIED**). Also: the faceplate low-speed ESD counted 20 lines where pinout v1 §8.2 has 21: TPD4E05U06 ×5 → ×6 (line 75); the ALS interrupt is now a 3.3 V line (line 60). §7 item 37 closed.
7. **Measurement:** one more link (36), L1 class at the MCLK_1V8 LDO input, so `blk.mclk` is attributed in full (line 137, power tree §4.12).
8. **Area (est.):** ≈ +15 mm² (est.: two VSSOP-8, one SOT-23-5, one SC70-6, passives).

## Changes in v1.8

The radio 1.8 V rail, LPDDR4X VDD1 and VDD2 against the rail map, and the feature-block gaps G1–G21 of the [block catalog](feature-blocks.md) §7, plus [ADR 0017](../adr/0017-fuel-gauge-wiring-sense-resistor-and-configuration.md) (fuel gauge, item 10) and [ADR 0018](../adr/0018-antennas-and-certification-route.md) (antennas, item 11). Calculations are in [power tree v0.2](power-tree-v0.md) (Changes in v0.2, §4.12, §4.13). Every new line names its block (RFC 0011) and its state per variant (`full` / `production`, BRD-053, ADR 0013). Assumptions about IO-domain voltages are marked "to reconcile with pinout v1".

1. **Radio 1.8 V power rail (lines 129–131): TI TPS62826DMQR** (the line 6 part) from VSYS_LV makes **vcc1v8_wl** for the Type 2EL AVDD18 pins (30, 31). AVDD18 is a power rail: 323–390 mA typical in Bluetooth and 802.15.4 transmit, 255 mA in Wi-Fi 5 GHz transmit (Murata E2B-09-1413 Tables 19–21), peaks above 1 A at start-up calibration (Embedded Artists M.2 datasheet PA4 pp. 8, 22; carrier, not module). BRD-049: 390 mA = 13 % of 3 A continuous; the 1.05 A peak puts the inductor at 1.51 A = 41 % of the 3.7 A minimum switch limit. Not on `vcc_1v8` (BUCK5): ampere-level transmit bursts would land on the rail that feeds the DAC VD, VL and VCP switch, and BUCK5 is off in suspend. EN follows vcc3v3_wl (RK809 SWOUT1), so one switch powers the module. The module's VIO and SD_VIO stay on `vcc_1v8` (VCCIO4 domain, to reconcile with pinout v1).
2. **LPDDR4X VDD1 and VDDQ bias moved to an always-on rail (lines 9, 132).** The rail map put the memory's VDD1 (1.8 V) and the TPS7A1006 BIAS on `vcc_1v8`, which is off in suspend (power tree §3), while self-refresh needs the DRAM supplies and the 0.6 V VDDQ (Rockchip HDG 1.1 p. 35 keeps VCC_DDR on in standby). Every RK3566 reference read keeps VDD1 always on: Radxa ZERO 3W v1.12 ties VCC1V8_DDR to VCCA1V8_PMU through a 0 Ω (R123), Pine64 Quartz64-A v2.0 through 0.1 Ω (R3807), Radxa ROCK 3C v1.4 from a dedicated LDO enabled by VCC3V3_PMU with the VCCA1V8_PMU link unfitted. The T1 follows the ZERO 3W: **vcc1v8_ddr = vcca1v8_pmu through a measurement link**, with a DNP TPS7A2018 footprint for the ROCK 3C topology; BIAS moves to vcc1v8_ddr.
3. **VDD2 checked:** `vcc_ddr` (RK809 BUCK3, external divider, VFB3 0.784 / 0.800 / 0.816 V, RK809 Rev 1.01 p. 17) at **47 kΩ / 120 kΩ 1 %** as on the ROCK 3C → 1.113 V nominal, ≈ 1.08–1.14 V worst case; the RK3566 needs 1.10 V on DDR_VDDQ with LPDDR4X (HDG 1.1 p. 23). The memory's VDD2 window and VDD1/VDD2 currents need Samsung's datasheet (NDA): **UNVERIFIED**. The TPS7A1006 headroom falls to ≈ 0.48 V at the low corner against the datasheet's VIN ≥ VOUT + 0.5 V condition (§7 item 33).
4. **DAC control-bus isolation (G2; line 133): TI SN74LVC2G66DCUR** analog switch on I2C2 SDA/SCL, VCC on vcca1v8_pmu, both controls driven by DAC_DIG_1V8 itself: the CS43198 pair joins the bus only while powered. 0 Ω bypass footprints (DNP). DAC_INT pull-up on DAC_DIG_1V8.
5. **MCLK interlock (G3) and PCIe reset gate (G14) (line 135): two SN74LVC1G57DCKR** (the line 98 family) wired as 2-input AND: the TPS7A2031 (MCLK_3V1) EN = `AUDIO_LN_EN` AND DAC_DIG_1V8, so the master clock never drives an unpowered XTI; `PCIE_PERST#` = SoC PERST# AND HAT power-good with the ≥ 100 ms RC.
6. **I2S series footprints (G2, G4, G21; line 134):** 0 Ω footprints at the DAC end of SDO0–SDO2, SCLK, LRCK, DSD clock and DSD data, and at the RP2354A tap branch. The RP2350's FT GPIOs accept up to 3.63 V with IOVDD = 0 V (absolute maximum, RP2350 datasheet), so the taps must use GPIO0–GPIO25, not the ADC pins (to reconcile with pinout v1).
7. **Always-on 1.8 V devices (G1, G5, G6, G15, G16, G19):** the IMU, TPM, NFC tag, board EEPROM, expander VCCI and the I2C2 pull-ups move to **vcca1v8_pmu** (RK809 LDO8, on in suspend), so nothing on I2C2 is unpowered while the bus is live; the PN7160 VDD(PAD) joins them. Budget: ≈ 160 mA (est.) = 39 % of LDO8's 400 mA rating, 79 % of its 200 mA low-headroom figure (power tree §4.13).
8. **PN7160 supply (G7; lines 50, 127):** VBAT and VDD(UP) from **VSYS_LV through an eighth TPS22919** (`NFC_PWR_EN`); 290 mA maximum in reader mode (PN7160 data sheet Rev. 3.4, Table 1) = 19 % of 1.5 A. VBAT 2.8–5.5 V operating, 6 V absolute (Table 1, Table 28): the 6.0–8.8 V battery path of v1.7 would have destroyed it. VEN and DWL_REQ on native 1.8 V GPIO, not the 3.3 V expander.
9. **Measurement (G10, G11, G12; lines 54, 136, 137):** one measurement link per block supply, **35 positions**, with Kelvin pads: a shunt in `full`, 0 Ω in `production` (the five ≥ 1.5 A positions keep their 5 mΩ shunt). INA236 positions defined (audio on a dedicated VSYS_LV branch, display on the TPS61169 input, VSYS, HAT) and a **fifth monitor, INA236BIDDFR at 0x48**, on the LMR33630C input (VSYS), so the audio block is attributed in full.
10. **Fuel gauge ([ADR 0017](../adr/0017-fuel-gauge-wiring-sense-resistor-and-configuration.md), Accepted; lines 42, 43, 120, §7 item 22):** PACK on the BQ25798 BAT node (switched side of the ship FET) instead of BAT+; sense resistor 5 mΩ → **3 mΩ Vishay WSL12063L000FEA** (same footprint; 31 mW at 3.2 A); storage and critical-battery power-off through gauge SHUTDOWN and the charger's shutdown mode (≈ 1 µA typical, 4.7 µA maximum board drain); golden-image fields; §7 item 22 (a)–(c) closed, (d) open.
11. **Antennas ([ADR 0018](../adr/0018-antennas-and-certification-route.md), Accepted; lines 56–58):** two Molex 146187-0050 → **one Molex 146153-0050** on ANT0 in Murata's shared configuration (on the FCC, ISED and Japan lists); the second U.FL (ANT1) is fitted in `full` only; matching in 0603 as Murata's reference, with the 10 pF pin 18–23 link and the dedicated-configuration positions DNP. One antenna instead of two.
12. **Other gaps:** I2C3 pull-ups on FP_3V3 (G8); a strap footprint on touch INT (G9); discharge footprints on vcc3v3_wl and vcc3v3_sd (G13); `HAPTIC_EN` (G20) and `NFC_PWR_EN` on expander spare lines with pull-downs; **16 GB eMMC option Samsung KLMAG1JETD-B041** (G17, line 138). G18 (2 GB LPDDR4X and SK hynix lands) stays open.
13. **Area (est.):** ≈ 300 mm² (est.): AVDD18 stage ≈ 30 mm², gates and switch ≈ 15 mm², fifth INA236 ≈ 15 mm², links and Kelvin pads ≈ 240 mm² (pads can go on the back side).

## Changes in v1.7

Adds the should parts confirmed on 2026-10-23 (BRD-057 NFC, BRD-058 haptics, BRD-059 key holder), chosen from primary sources. Prototypes populate every option (BRD-053).

1. **Key holder (line 122): Infineon SLB9673**, TPM 2.0 on I2C2 at 0x2E, the only I2C TPM named in mainline `tpm_tis_i2c`; ST ST33KTPM2 as second source. Secure elements (NXP SE050, Microchip ATECC608B) were not chosen: no kernel interface, vendor middleware only, whereas a TPM works with the standard Debian tooling. It backs the keystore of OS-032 (blackbird-os ADR 0013).
2. **Haptics (lines 123–124): TI DRV2605L** (mainline since v4.0 or earlier, closed loop, auto-resonance) with a **Vybronics VG1040003D** coin LRA; DRV2624/2625 have no mainline driver, CS40L50 has no authorized source and needs firmware outside linux-firmware.
3. **NFC (lines 125–128):** a **dynamic tag, NXP NTAG I2C plus**, for tap-to-pair: no driver, readable with the T1 off, nothing transmitting near the audio chain. The **NXP PN7160** reader is the population option for T1-to-T1 hand-off (fitted on prototypes). Mainline Linux has no NFC card emulation, so the tag is the phone path in every variant.
4. **Kernel:** Debian arm64 builds neither `TCG_TIS_I2C` nor `INPUT_DRV260X_HAPTICS`: both ship as DKMS modules or in OpenDAP's kernel configuration (OS-023) until Debian enables them; `NFC_NXP_NCI_I2C` is already `=m`.
5. **I2C2 M0 addresses** added: 0x28 (PN7160), 0x2E (TPM), 0x55 (tag), 0x5A (haptic driver); all free on that bus.

## Changes in v1.6

Applies [ADR 0015](../adr/0015-amplifier-rails-from-vsys.md) (Accepted, 2026-10-22; supersedes the rail items of ADR 0012) from the [output-stage simulation](../../sim/output-stage/README.md), [ADR 0014](../adr/0014-two-cell-battery.md) item 6, and the [VSYS buck and MaskROM simulations](../../sim/README.md).

1. **−7 V pre-rail (new lines 116–119): TI LMR33630CRNXR** inverting buck-boost from VSYS (SNVA856B topology), with a Coilcraft XGL4020-222MEC (2.2 µH; XAL4020-222MEC second source), its capacitors and feedback, and an EN level shifter (DMG1012T-7 + DMP3099L-7, SNVA856B Figure 7-1). Simulated: 47 % of the high-side minimum limit at the full-scale peak, 55 % at the LDO current-limit case, 48 % of the voltage rating. LMR33620CRNXR is the second source.
2. **TPS65131 (lines 22–24, 111): +7 V boost only.** The inverter half is unused (ENN, PSN to GND; INN on VIN; VNEG, FBN to AGND; OUTN open; connection to confirm with TI). One DFE252012F-4R7M, one PMEG2010AEH, R3/R4, C10, CN and 4 × 4.7 µF removed. The boost runs at 56–78 % in operation and ≈ 99 % only in the positive LDOs' maximum-trip case (bench item).
3. **±6 V regulators doubled (lines 25–26, 109–110):** 2 × LT3045 and 2 × LT3094 in parallel, RSET 2 × 60.4 kΩ, CSET 2 × 0.47 µF, **RILIM 523 Ω / 13.0 kΩ** per device (pair limits 516–631 / 519–646 mA against the 457 mA peak), 20 mΩ ballast per output.
4. **Sequencing:** `HPA_RAIL_EN` enables the TPS65131 boost and, through the level shifter, the LMR33630C; `HPA_LDO_EN` follows **≥ 10 ms** later; the OPA1622 EN last (power tree §6.2).
5. **Ship FET (new line 120): TI CSD17575Q3**, the ship FET of TI's BQ25798EVM (SLUUCB5E); 27 A continuous = 6.8× the 4 A of the brief.
6. **MaskROM (lines 35, 78, 97, 98):** TPS3839G18DBZR → **TPS3808G01DBVR** on vcca1v8_pmu (280 kΩ / 100 kΩ, CT 390 nF, RESET pull-up 100 kΩ: window 1.21–3.43 s, never at resume); PWRON_N pull-up 20 kΩ → **100 kΩ**; eMMC_D0 series resistor 100 Ω → **1 kΩ**; 100 kΩ gate pull-down on the DMG1012T fitted (qty 2 → 3 with the level shifter). Consequence: MaskROM through the buttons needs a full power-off and an empty or unbootable SD slot (t1 controls spec §1.2).
7. **TPS62903 5.1 V output capacitor (new line 121):** one 22 µF with ≥ 12 µF effective at 5.1 V: Murata GRM32ER71E226KE15L (25 V X7R 1210). Phase margin at VSYS 6.0 V stays a bench item (§7 item 27).
8. **Correction, line 114:** the TPS62136 inductor peak at 2.0 A is **3.03 A** (63 % of the minimum limit, 40 % of Isat), not 2.41 A.
9. **Battery worst case (power tree §4.7):** the −7 V rail now draws from VSYS directly (2.12 W at the full-scale sine, simulated) instead of through the pre-regulator; the combined worst case falls from ≈ 20.9 W to **≈ 20.6 W, ≈ 3.1 A at VSYS 6.6 V**: the 3.2 A design point, the 5 A fuse (62 %), the 4.0 A pack criterion (78 %) and the Micro-Fit contact (62 %) all hold.
10. **§7 item 26 closed** by ADR 0015.

## Changes in v1.5

Applies [ADR 0014](../adr/0014-two-cell-battery.md) (Accepted, 2026-10-22; supersedes ADR 0011). The BQ25798, BQ28Z610-R1, TPS62136 and TPS62903 figures come from their datasheets (SLUSDV2C, SLUSE07B, SLVSDV2, SLVSES3A), the inductors from Coilcraft Doc 806, 1302 and 1529.

1. **Two-cell pack (line 46).** The T1 runs on a made-to-order 2S1P Li-ion pack, a T1-038 exception like the cover glass, accepted only against the ADR 0014 item 2 criteria (≥ 4.0 A continuous, PCM trip 4.0–5.0 A with balancing, lead and plug ≥ 4.6 A per pole, UN 38.3, two independent makers). The Adafruit 328 and Adafruit 2011 are removed: the Adafruit 328 is no longer a T1 pack. Bring-up uses a bench supply or a protected 2S pack on the bench.
2. **Charger (lines 39–40): BQ25895RTWR → BQ25798RQMR** (1–4S buck-boost, NVDC, 6 A continuous discharge; I2C1 at 0x6B). VSYS becomes 6.0–8.8 V. The XAL4020-102MEC stays as its 1 µH inductor (72 % of Isat at the 5 A ISW rating, ≈ 40 % at the design point). Driver: mainline `bq257xx` with the BQ25798 ID sent upstream, DKMS meanwhile (ADR 0014 item 5).
3. **Pre-regulator (new lines 113–115): TI TPS62136RGXR**, VSYS → **3.8 V VSYS_LV**, with a Coilcraft XGL4020-152MEC (1.5 µH). Every part the 1S tree fed from VSYS moves to VSYS_LV unchanged : RK809-5 VCC1–VCC4 (line 2), RK860-0 (3), TPS62826 (6), TPS65131 (22), TPS62840 (27), TPS7A2031 (29), MAX98357A (32), the DAC_VP TPS22919 (50), TPS61169 (51) and LP5521 (105). The fixed 3.8 V input removes the low-battery corner of the TPS65131 (inverter 79 % → 68 % of its minimum switch limit) and of the MCLK LDO (0.7 V headroom).
4. **HAT and USB-C 5.1 V (lines 91–92): TPS61089RNRR boost → TI TPS62903RPJR buck** (1.6 A = 53 % of 3 A), inductor XAL4020-152MEC → **Coilcraft XGL4020-102MEC**. **HAT_3V3 (lines 38, 7): TLV62569DBVR → a second TPS62903RPJR** (33 %), inductor DFE252012F-2R2M → XGL4020-102MEC. One part number for both bucks, set by a VSET resistor (5.1 V or 3.3 V).
5. **Gauge (lines 42–43, 101): MAX17055EWL+T → TI BQ28Z610DRZR-R1** (1–2S, gauge only: the pack carries protection and balancing), sense resistor RL0603FR-070R01L (10 mΩ) → **Vishay WSL12065L000FEA (5 mΩ, 0.25 W)**. Line 101 (optional MAX17055 thermistor) is removed; the board NTC (line 48) moves to the gauge TS1 and the charger TS takes the pack NTC.
6. **Battery fuse (line 104): 045102.5MRL (interim) / 0451007.MRL → Littelfuse 0451005.MRL (5 A)**, same NANO2 footprint: 3.2 A worst continuous case = 64 % (ADR 0014; a first v1.5 draft carried the 4 A 0451004.MRL, 80 % at 3.2 A).
7. **Battery connector (line 47): JST S2B-PH-SM4-TB → Molex Micro-Fit 3.0 43650-0400** (4 circuits: P+, P−, mid-cell tap, NTC; right-angle through-hole): 3.2 A worst case = 64 % of 5 A per contact (2.4 A = 48 %). The keyed plug cannot take a 1S JST-PH pack.
8. **Full output budget on battery (power tree §4.7):** 15.7 W from VSYS = **≈ 2.4 A at VSYS 6.6 V** (3.3 V per cell, the shed point), half the 1S figure; path drop ≈ 0.1 V and ≈ 0.23 W (est.). The interim output ceiling of v1.3 (power tree §4.11) is removed with a compliant pack (ADR 0014 item 3). With full-scale balanced headphones on top (≈ 3.2 A, §7 item 17) the battery path is sized for the combined worst case (ADR 0014): pack ≥ 4.0 A, PCM trip 4.0–5.0 A, lead ≥ 4.6 A per pole, 5 A fuse.
9. **Board EEPROM (line 17):** the notes said 0x57; the AT24C02D-STUM-T stays the SOT23 part at **0x50** on I2C2. The HAT ID EEPROM, also at 0x50, is on its own bus (I2C4 behind the PCA9306, line 94), so there is no conflict. This matches D1 and the §4.2 address map.
10. **Debian (§3):** `MFD_BQ257XX` and `CHARGER_BQ257XX` not set (DKMS for line 39); `BATTERY_BQ27XXX=m` but `BATTERY_BQ27XXX_I2C` not set and not overridden on arm64 (DKMS for line 42; ADR 0014 item 5). `CHARGER_BQ25890` and `BATTERY_MAX17042` no longer apply.

## Changes in v1.4

Applies the hat-standard [HAT interface v1](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/specs/interface-v1.md) (Draft, 2026-10-22; [ADR 0002](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/adr/0002-interface-v1-native-high-speed-management-over-usb.md), [ADR 0003](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/adr/0003-compatibility-levels.md)). The RK3566 mapping and the freed balls are in the [pinout v1](pinout-v1.md). No power line changes: the HAT rails, eFuses, TVS and the DF40 power connector stay as in v1.3.

1. **HAT signal connector (line 66):** stays the 100-position DF40 pair (ADR 0002). 72 of 96 usable contacts are assigned (11 pairs + 13 grounds, 23 low-speed, 4 analog, 5 low-speed grounds, 5 reserved); 24 freed positions are spare and left unconnected.
2. **HAT pogo pins (line 67): ~128 → 96** (L3 field, 4 × 24; an L1 piece would carry 32). The P70-1000045R pad does not fit the 1.27 mm pitch assumed earlier (D36).
3. **HAT high-speed ESD (line 68):** 12 → 11 pairs (the second USB 2 port is removed); still 6 TPD4E05U06, 22 of 24 channels used.
4. **HAT low-speed and analog ESD (line 69): TPD4E05U06 ×12 → ×8, TPD1E10B06 ×4 → ×2.** 29 low-speed lines (23 + `HAT_DET#` + 5 reserved) instead of 46; line in removed.
5. **HAT audio translators (line 95): 3 parts → 2.** The port runs on the DAC's clock domain with fixed directions: SN74AXC4T774 for BCLK, FS and DOUT, SN74AXC8T245 for DIN0–DIN3. **SN74AXC2T245RSWR removed** (§8 item 7).
6. **New line 112 (Open): HAT `I2S_MCLK` buffer** from the active MCLK oscillator, because the HAT clock now comes from the DAC's domain, not from a SoC I2S controller (§7 item 19).
7. **GPIO expander (line 93):** carries no HAT line any more (`GPIO5`–`GPIO7` and `CAM_RST#` removed from the interface); quantity unchanged.
8. **IO domains (§4.2):** VCCIO1 keeps HAT SWD, `HAT_RST#` and the PCIe sidebands; VCCIO7 keeps HAT I2C5; I2S2 leaves VCCIO6.

## Changes in v1.3

Applies [ADR 0012](../adr/0012-headphone-output-stage.md) (Accepted, 2026-10-22) and closes D27 and D30 (§9). The LT3045 and LT3094 figures now come from their datasheets (LT3045 Rev. D, LT3094 Rev. B).

1. **Headphone amplifiers (line 19): four → six OPA1622** (BRD-022 amended). Both halves of one package run in parallel on each balanced leg; the 3.5 mm output keeps two packages with paralleled halves. Expected (desk estimate): ≈ 240 mW SE and ≈ 640 mW balanced into 32 Ω.
2. **Gain network (lines 37, 107, 108):** one difference amplifier per half, R1 = R3 = 1.00 kΩ, R2 = R4 = 1.62 kΩ (0.1 % thin film), 100 pF C0G, gain 1.62; a 1.0 Ω ballast resistor per half outside the loop. The CS43198 runs at `+1dB_EN` = 0 (−115 dB THD+N typ instead of −105 dB).
3. **Amplifier rails (lines 22, 25, 26, 109–111): ±5.0 V → ±6.0 V.** TPS7A4901DGNR and TPS7A3001DGNR **removed** (183 % and 138 % of their ratings at the 275 mA worst continuous load); LT3045EDD#TRPBF and LT3094EDD#TRPBF at ±6.04 V (RSET 60.4 kΩ, CSET 0.47 µF, ≈ 400 mA programmed limit), 55 % of their 500 mA rating. TPS65131 pre-rails raised from ±6.0 V to ±7.0 V (R1 475 kΩ / R2 100 kΩ, R3 576 kΩ / R4 100 kΩ). This amends the rail line of ADR 0009, as ADR 0012 states.
4. **TPS65131 margin (BRD-049, power tree §4.10.1):** inverter at 72–79 % of its 1.8 A minimum switch limit (VIN 3.5–3.0 V), boost at 48–56 %. Inside the 80 % rule, at its edge at VIN 3.0 V: conditional on simulation before layout freeze (ADR 0012). New D31: the TPS65131 inductors' 2.1 A rating sits below the 2.2 A maximum switch limit.
5. **Interim battery (power tree §4.11):** the full-scale balanced 32 Ω sine draws ≈ 4.8 W from VSYS (1.61 A at 3.0 V), at the Adafruit 328's 1.6 A path budget by itself. Blackbird OS must hold the battery current at ≤ 1.6 A by limiting the headphone level while that pack is fitted (requirement for Blackbird OS).
6. **vcc3v3_sys inductor (line 106, D27): DFE252012F-R47M → Würth 744373240047** (TI-listed, 6.8 A in TI's table, IR 8.7 A and Isat 10.4 A at 10 % drop on the Würth page). Line 4 keeps the DFE252012F-R47M for vdd_cpu only (qty 2 → 1).
7. **Quiescent cost:** the SE output's headphone rails draw ≈ 256 mW from VSYS at idle instead of ≈ 180 mW (+76 mW); the balanced output ≈ 464 mW (power tree §4.9). Board area ≈ +80–110 mm² (est.).

## Changes in v1.2

Power margin rule used here (BRD-049): continuous load ≤ 80 % of the rating for converters, regulators, switches and fuses (fuses ≤ 75 %), ≤ 70 % for connector contacts with all contacts loaded; the worst-case trip of the protecting current limiter ≤ 100 % of the rating. The full check is in [power tree §4.10](power-tree-v0.md#410-power-margin-check-bom-v12).

1. **vcc3v3_sys buck (line 6): TLV62569DBVR (2 A) → TI TPS62826DMQR (3 A)**, 2.4–5.5 V in, 100 % duty cycle, power-good output (SLVSEF9I). Peak 1.85 A (est.) is 62 % of 3 A and the inductor peak 2.25 A is 61 % of its 3.7 A minimum switch limit; v1.1 ran at 80–90 % of the TLV62569. The pin- and BOM-compatible TPS62827DMQR (4 A) is the drop-in if the schematic re-sum exceeds 2.4 A. Its 0.47 µH inductor is the DFE252012F-R47M already on line 4 (TI's recommended L–C matrix); the TPS62A03 fallback is dropped.
2. **HAT power connector (line 66): DF40C-30 → DF40C-50 pair** (46 usable contacts). At 0.3 A per contact the 30-pin could not carry `HAT_5V` and `HAT_3V3` with their returns at 70 % (HAT_3V3 sat at 96 % of four contacts at its eFuse ceiling). The allocation (HAT_5V ×12, HAT_3V3 ×6, GND ×22, HAT_DET# ×1, reserved ×5) now comes from the hat-standard [HAT interface v0](https://github.com/opendap-project/hat-standard/blob/main/docs/specs/interface-v0.md) (Draft).
3. **HAT signal connector (line 66): 2 reserved contacts** (HCS-022) by dropping `CAM_PWDN` (expander line freed, line 93) and the optional HAT output on the DAC clock (one AXC bit freed, line 95). Contact positions are owned by the hat-standard spec; the board maps the RK3566 onto it ([pinout v1](pinout-v1.md)).
4. **Battery (lines 46–47) and new board fuse (line 104):** [ADR 0011](../adr/0011-battery-path-rated-for-the-full-output-budget.md) (Proposed) defines the full output budget on battery (4.8 A from the cell at VSYS 3.3 V) and the pack acceptance criteria (≥ 6 A continuous, PCM trip ≥ 6 A, lead and connector ≥ 6.9 A per pole). **No off-the-shelf pack meeting them was found**, so the Adafruit 328 stays as the interim pack with the 1.6 A budget, now backed by a 2.5 A fuse; BRD-050 is not met yet (§7 item 3).
5. **Battery-path parts sized for the full budget:** BQ25895 6 A continuous discharge checked (80 %); input current limit capped at 15 W and 2.0 A (line 39); TPS2121 current limit set to 2.0–3.0 A (line 45); VSYS shunt ≥ 1 W (line 54); gauge sense resistor sized per pack (line 43).
6. **TPS61089 current limit (lines 91–92):** the datasheet allows the limit to sit 0.8 A below the calculated value (SLVSD38C §9.2.2.3), so 200 kΩ gives 4.35 A minimum, not 4.6 A; the 3.7 A peak was 85 % of it. RILIM 200 kΩ → **182 kΩ** (5.66 A typ, 4.86 A min): peak at 76 %.
7. **Status LED (lines 79, 105): RGB** (FPT-025). Everlight 19-337/R6GHBHC-A01/2T (red AlGaInP, green and blue InGaN, VF ≤ 3.8 V at 20 mA) on the faceplate, driven from the board by a **TI LP5521** I2C RGB driver whose 1.5× charge pump keeps the LED supply at ≥ 4.29 V down to VSYS = 3.0 V (0.29 V margin over the maximum VF and driver saturation at 20 mA). Mainline `leds-lp5521` (DKMS on Debian). The faceplate connector carries LED_R, LED_G, LED_B (2 spares used, 5 left, line 71). The yellow-green v1.1 part is withdrawn.
8. **HP-amp +5 V LDO (line 25): margin finding.** The worst continuous sine (balanced, 32 Ω, both channels, 122 mA) is 81 % of the TPS7A4901's 150 mA.
